Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
UNCLEAR · open: counted as not met until a person checks it
The hospital's documents did not let us decide this for certain. A person will check it by hand. Until then, the grade counts it as not met.
“BHN will assume patients are eligible for financial assistance for residents of New York State, if they reside within the hospital’s primary service area of Central New York (Little Falls, Delhi, Herkimer, Oneonta, Cobleskill, and Cooperstown, NY).” document page 2
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

A: yes - the 2026 policy defines the PSA as 'Central New York (Little Falls, Delhi, Herkimer, Oneonta, Cobleskill, and Cooperstown, NY)' with a residence rule; the 2014 guide (46e36a10) instead says 'any Bassett Healthcare Network patient may apply regardless of the location of their residence.' B: the two documents do not give the same area; the policy (6/2/2026) is the most recent PSA definition and it is stated only in towns, not counties. C: the current PSA is not statewide or wider. D: the hospital's address is One Atwell Road, Cooperstown, NY 13326, but no document states which county Cooperstown is in (the guide lists an Otsego County DSS office but does not say the hospital is in it), so the hospital's county cannot be confirmed from the documents. E-G: cannot be checked - the PSA is given only as a list of towns and the rules say not to convert towns to counties myself; the DOH chart of assigned non-contiguous counties was also not provided. Per the reading rule for town-only PSAs, the answer is UNCLEAR.

No test of savings or property
Materials On Website
NO · not met
This hospital's documents ask about savings or other assets, for example bank statements, when deciding who gets help.
“• Copies of last two bank statements” document page 4
Rule: Under the law, who qualifies depends on income: patients with income below 400% of the federal poverty level are presumed to qualify. The law sets no test of savings or property, and the state's application form does not ask about them. The application process must not be unduly burdensome.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(e)
Model's reasoning

A: yes - the policy's list of what those seeking assistance "will be asked to provide" (page 4) includes "Copies of last two bank statements"; a request for bank account statements is evidence of an asset check. The policy (page 5) also says internal criteria "may include review of previous account history and a credit check(s)". The application itself asks only for proof of income (pay stub, employer letter, tax return), which is not an asset check, but the policy's bank-statement request is enough. B: no document says assets are counted in the eligibility formula, but check A already shows an asset request. NO.

No test of savings or property
Materials On Website
NO · not met
This hospital's documents ask about savings or other assets, for example bank statements, when deciding who gets help.
“Verification of Income should be provided with the application. Acceptable verification may include: i. Prior Year Tax Returns ii. Current Pay Stubs iii. Written verification of wages from Employer iv. Unemployment Letter v. Copy of Social Security check vi. Bank Statement” document page 9
Rule: Under the law, who qualifies depends on income: patients with income below 400% of the federal poverty level are presumed to qualify. The law sets no test of savings or property, and the state's application form does not ask about them. The application process must not be unduly burdensome.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(e)
Model's reasoning

A: yes - the policy's list of acceptable verification (Exhibit A, p9) includes 'Bank Statement', which per the stated guidance is evidence of an asset check. B: yes - the policy also addresses assets in the eligibility determination: p2 'Assets shall not be considered in determining assistance for patients at or below 200% of the Federal Poverty Guidelines' and p10 lists assets (primary residence, retirement accounts, college savings, motor vehicle) that are 'not to be considered' for the 201-300% categories, implying assets are reviewed for those patients. Pay stubs/tax returns alone would be income proof, but the bank statement request and asset listings make this NO.

Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
UNCLEAR · open: counted as not met until a person checks it
The hospital's documents did not let us decide this for certain. A person will check it by hand. Until then, the grade counts it as not met.
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)

Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
UNCLEAR · open: counted as not met until a person checks it
The hospital's documents did not let us decide this for certain. A person will check it by hand. Until then, the grade counts it as not met.
“For emergent and medically necessary health care services, a patient has to be a resident in The Guthrie Clinic Service area, which consists of the following counties: Broome, Bradford, Sullivan, Susquehanna, Tioga, and Wyoming in Pennsylvania and Chemung, Cortland, Schuyler, Steuben, Tioga, and Tompkins, in New York.” document page 5
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

A: yes, the PSA is defined - the FY27 policy (p.5, above) and the FY23 NHSC application (p.2) each give a county list as the area whose residents can get assistance. B: both name, in Pennsylvania: Bradford, Sullivan, Susquehanna, Tioga, Wyoming; and in New York: Broome, Chemung, Cortland, Schuyler, Steuben, Tioga, Tompkins (the policy places Broome in its Pennsylvania clause, an apparent typo; the NHSC application adds a six-month residency rule). C: the area is not all of New York State and not the five NYC counties. D: I cannot tell from the documents which county the hospital is in - The Guthrie Clinic spans several hospitals in New York and Pennsylvania (Corning Hospital, Cortland Medical Center, Guthrie Lourdes Hospital, Robert Packer Hospital, Troy Community Hospital, RPH at Towanda Campus) and no document states any hospital's county; the only address given is a mailing address in Sayre, PA. E/F/G: because the home county cannot be determined from the documents, I cannot verify that the required contiguous counties are present; the counties named are reported in psa_counties for a person to check. The DOH chart of assigned non-contiguous counties was not provided and was not checked.

Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
NO · not met

“Thompson Health also provides Financial Assistance to patients, who reside in our primary service areas, including Ontario, Wayne and Yates counties, and receive medically necessary inpatient or outpatient services that a physician, exercising prudent clinical judgment, would order or provide for a patient.” document page 1
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

The summary (559562f2) refers to 'the Hospital's primary service area' without naming counties, so it does not itself define it. B: named PSA counties: Ontario, Wayne, Yates (policy only). C: the PSA is not statewide and not the five NYC counties, so continue. D: the hospital is in Canandaigua (the policy gives 73 Buffalo St, Canandaigua, NY 14424), which is in Ontario County; Ontario is named first in the PSA. E: not a NYC hospital. F: the hospital's own county, Ontario, is named. G: counties bordering Ontario County are Monroe, Wayne, Seneca, Yates, Livingston and Steuben (I am certain of these); only Wayne and Yates are named - Monroe, Seneca, Livingston and Steuben are missing. Per the note, the county list for non-emergency care is judged even though emergency care is offered statewide. Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
NO · not met
This hospital's service area, as its documents describe it, leaves out at least one county the state's rules require.
“Service Area includes ZIP codes in Orange, Sullivan, and Ulster Counties.” document page 2
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

A: yes - the Financial Assistance Policy (b466cd10 p2) and the identical Billing and Collection Policy (a91f6ac7 p2) define the service area, and the policy (p2) and summary (83fac2cc p1) give a residence rule: New York State residents for emergency services, and patients who 'reside in Garnet Health’s primary service areas' (or work/visit there) for all other services. B: the policy/billing policy name ZIP codes in Orange, Sullivan and Ulster Counties with 'a primary focus on Orange County and Sullivan County'; the summary references the primary service area without naming counties. C: the area is not all of New York State for non-emergency care, so the county list is judged. D: the hospitals are at 707 E. Main St., Middletown, NY (Orange County) and 68 Harris-Bushville Rd., Harris, NY (Sullivan County); the web page confirms 'locations across Orange and Sullivan counties'. E: not a New York City hospital. F: the hospital's own counties (Orange, Sullivan) are named. G: but Orange County also borders Rockland, Dutchess and Putnam counties, and Sullivan County also borders Delaware County; none of these contiguous counties is included. The PSA therefore omits counties the 2007 rule requires (Rockland and Delaware with certainty; Dutchess and Putnam across the Hudson River). Answer NO. The DOH chart of assigned non-contiguous counties was not provided, so those were not checked.

“Lewis County General Hospital provides health care services for all who need it regardless of their ability to pay.” document page 1
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.

Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
UNCLEAR · open: counted as not met until a person checks it
The hospital's documents did not let us decide this for certain. A person will check it by hand. Until then, the grade counts it as not met.
“Noyes also provides Financial Assistance to patients, who reside in our primary service areas, including Livingston and Steuben counties, and receive medically necessary inpatient or outpatient services that a physician, exercising prudent clinical judgment, would order or provide for a patient.” document page 1
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

A: yes - the policy defines the PSA for non-emergency care as 'our primary service areas, including Livingston and Steuben counties'; emergency services are offered to New York State residents. The summary (ec7be49e) refers to 'the Hospital's primary service area' without defining it. B: named as the PSA: Livingston and Steuben counties (policy only). The Noyes web page lists DSS contacts for Livingston, Steuben, Allegany, Yates, Ontario and Wyoming counties, but as resources, not as the PSA. C: the PSA is not statewide for non-emergency care, so the county list is what is judged. D: no document gives Noyes's own address, city or county (the only address given is the UR Medicine Affiliate Billing Office at 73 Buffalo St, Canandaigua), so I cannot tell from the documents which county the hospital is in. E: the documents do not place the hospital in New York City. F and G: cannot be checked without knowing the hospital's county. The list is introduced by 'including', so it may not be exhaustive. Answer UNCLEAR and a person will check; the DOH chart of assigned non-contiguous counties was not provided, so those were not checked.

Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
UNCLEAR · open: counted as not met until a person checks it
The hospital's documents did not let us decide this for certain. A person will check it by hand. Until then, the grade counts it as not met.
“For emergent and medically necessary health care services, a patient has to be a resident in The Guthrie Clinic Service area, which consists of the following counties: Broome, Bradford, Sullivan, Susquehanna, Tioga, and Wyoming in Pennsylvania and Chemung, Cortland, Schuyler, Steuben, Tioga, and Tompkins, in New York.” document page 5
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

A: yes, the PSA is defined - the FY27 policy (p.5, above) and the FY23 NHSC application (p.2) each give a county list as the area whose residents can get assistance. B: both name, in Pennsylvania: Bradford, Sullivan, Susquehanna, Tioga, Wyoming; and in New York: Broome, Chemung, Cortland, Schuyler, Steuben, Tioga, Tompkins (the policy places Broome in its Pennsylvania clause, an apparent typo; the NHSC application adds a six-month residency rule). C: the area is not all of New York State and not the five NYC counties. D: I cannot tell from the documents which county the hospital is in - The Guthrie Clinic spans several hospitals in New York and Pennsylvania (Corning Hospital, Cortland Medical Center, Guthrie Lourdes Hospital, Robert Packer Hospital, Troy Community Hospital, RPH at Towanda Campus) and no document states any hospital's county; the only address given is a mailing address in Sayre, PA. E/F/G: because the home county cannot be determined from the documents, I cannot verify that the required contiguous counties are present; the counties named are reported in psa_counties for a person to check. The DOH chart of assigned non-contiguous counties was not provided and was not checked.

No test of savings or property
Materials On Website
UNCLEAR · open: counted as not met until a person checks it
The hospital's documents did not let us decide this for certain. A person will check it by hand. Until then, the grade counts it as not met.
Rule: Under the law, who qualifies depends on income: patients with income below 400% of the federal poverty level are presumed to qualify. The law sets no test of savings or property, and the state's application form does not ask about them. The application process must not be unduly burdensome.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(e)
Note: the model answered NO, but its quote was not found in the document, so it is open
Model's reasoning

A: yes - the posted FY23 'Financial Assistance Application for NHSC Sites' (4a255902) requests 'Bank statement (most recent detailed)' and 'Heath Savings Account (HSA) Statement showing current balance' (pp. 1 and 3) and asks for statements from savings accounts, stocks, bonds and annuities (p.3). B: yes - the current FY27 policy requires patients to exhaust Health Savings Account benefits and reserves the right to review the HSA account balance when determining assistance (quoted, p.5). Both are evidence that assets are requested and considered.

Help for incomes up to 400% of the poverty level
Application
YES · met
This hospital's financial assistance reaches incomes of at least 400% of the federal poverty level.
“Patients with household income greater than 200% but less than or equal to 400% of FPL may be eligible for discounted care.” document page 1
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)
Model's reasoning

A: the highest income level receiving assistance is 400% of the FPL - stated in the FY27 policy (p.5, 'greater than 200% but less than or equal to 400% of FPL'), the FY27 plain language summary (quoted), the uniform application's 301% - 400% discount band, and the 2026 guidelines sheet (sliding scale to 400%). The policy additionally makes under-insured patients whose paid medical costs exceed 10% of income eligible 'regardless of federal poverty level limits'. B: not needed (percentages given). C: 400% is at least 400%, so YES. The older NHSC form states no maximum, so there is no unreconciled conflict on this point.
